SEVERAL ASTRONOMICAL DEFINITIONS
‘What is the Horizon?

When we stand in open counsey and look around, we quickly note, tha:
the earth's surface appears as a round plane, in whose centre We ace ourselves.
This circle of vision is called the Apparent Horizon, Everything seems to be
bordered by the bow] of heaven, which appears to tese upon ic. The True Horizon
is a plane, parallel co the one just explained, buc running through the center
of the earth. Thus, this true horizon is always a greatest circle, of the heavenly
bowl proper, and divides that bow! into cwo equal parts.

Ler us assume we put into the place of the observer, of into the centre of
the horizon a vertical line. We then obtain with this imaginary line the Axis
of the Horizon, To obtain the idea of an axis, we extend the line on both sides
through che earth up to the heaven. Where this line couches the heaven we have
the Poles of the Horizon: sbove, the North Pole, called the Zenith, below, the
South Pole, called Nadir. Circles drawn through ‘these cwo points, whose plane
passes through die ceatce of the eatth, are called Vertical Circles, (See Fig. ?
NZSZ’) on page 40.

The distance of a Star E from the horizon or the angle that is formed by
2 line drawn from the Observer towards the Star, is called the Alsitude of the
Star (EME).

‘The angle which complements this alciude to a right angle (co 90°), is
called the Zenith Distance (EMZ). All points of equal altitude lie on one and
the same Alticude Circle, such as EEC, These alticude circles, since the sky appeacs
like a bowl, become smaller, the more we imagine them removed from the
horizon. A point in the horizon has an altitude of 0°, whereas the point of the
Zenith has an altitude of 90°. All other points lie berween 0* and 90°.

‘The horizon is divided inco four parts, the four points of the compass. When
we look at noon.towacds the Sun and imagine through chis Sun 2 semi-circle
standing vertically on the horizon, the point that aus this horizon is Midday
for the south point; opposite of this point is che Midnight point or the north
point, towards the left, 90° away from che former, is Morning or the East point
And opposite to it, Evening or the West point. In these latter poincs the Sun
rises and sets on Mach 2Ist and September 23:d each year.

The arcs between these points are, of course, still further divided, We
obtain NE, SE, NW and SW. Between the sub-division points we still heve
smaller pacts, but they ace only used for navigation. They have no practical value

Moments of Beginning

‘When one sits quietly at home in the evening, reads a book or makes plans
for che next day, the ring of the door bell often times is a sign: an important
aspect has artived. Some desicable or undesicable visitor (Une aspect itself) is at
the door, Mark that time down immediately for future reference, You might need
just that moment, because that door bell brings about a “change of scenery"!
‘These are moments upon which to cast horoscopes latet 00, if one is needed, to
find out the effects of the visit. This is why an astrologer must be sure to have
4 costect time piece, besides a small note book, wherein he enters such momencs
immediately. It does not mean that we have to cast horoscopes each time someone
arrives, but, one never knows, what an evenc brings forth. Things often develop
from small’ insignificant meetings and if the particular moment of the first meet-
ing has noe been recorded properly as to time and day, no further moment will
ever bring forth the correct horoscope.

Ic is always the first cause chac is she beginning! For example, you intend
to teke’a ctip t0 California during the summer. It is not the time you leave your
home and start the ttip, thae counts so much, as it is the time when the matter
vias first discussed. It might have happened the following way that caused you
to make a trip: During a Spring storm some motorist came to your house ar nigh
asking for gasoline. You helped him. Ac that time he told you his “hard-lack’
story how he left Boston on his way so Los Angeles, how he now ran ouc of gas.
‘You may even have offered him a cup of coffee and duriag the talic that ensued,
he always spoke, that he was heading West. Later on, some friends might talk
“California” and you tell about that fellow who recendly was ac your house. From
this talk you start to get “feelings” for California. These feelings gradually get

just र० “look” the roads over, Later, you decide upon your trip to California aad
finally, even make the trip. ‘The moment co start 2 horoscope for such a trip is

not the day you scart the trip, but the moment when thac fellow came co your
door to ask for gasoline.

All other cases of a similar nature must be reduced to theit point of actual
beginning, the origin.

Let me show you why such proceduce is correct. Supposing, in time of peace
we make a trip to Europe. It would be all wrong to make a horoscope for the
sroment the boar leaves for Europe, because thousands of ocher passengers would

may never be used, but then, there will be one date marked that is like 2 God—
